Calderón-Zygmund theory of nonlocal parabolic equations with discontinuous coefficients Thanks: S. Byun was supported by NRF-2022R1A2C1009312, K. Kim and D. Kumar were supported by NRF-2021R1A4A1027378.
Sun-Sig Byun, Kyeongbae Kim and Deepak Kumar

Abstract
. We prove Calderón-Zygmund type estimates of weak solutions to non-homogeneous nonlocal parabolic equations under a minimal regularity requirement on kernel coefficients. In particular, the right-hand side is presented by a sum of fractional Laplacian type data and a non-divergence type data. Interestingly, even though the kernel coefficients are discontinuous, we obtain a significant increment of fractional differentiability for the solutions, which is not observed in the corresponding local parabolic equations.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35A01, 35B65, 35D30, 35R05, 35R09.

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Overview

In this paper, we study higher regularity properties for weak solutions to the following non-homogeneous nonlocal parabolic equation:

(1.1)

u_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}u=(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f+g\quad\text{ in }\Omega_{T}\equiv\Omega\times(0,T),

where
s\in(0,1)
,
T>0
and
\Omega
is an open and bounded set in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
n\geq 2
.
The nonlocal operators appearing in problem (
1.1
) are defined by

\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}u(x,t)=\mathrm{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Phi\left(\frac{u(x,t)-u(y,t)}{|x-y|^{s}}\right)\frac{A(x,y,t)}{|x-y|^{n+s}}\,dy

and

(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f(x,t)=\mathrm{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(f(x,t)-f(y,t))\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n+s}}\,dy.

Here,
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(0,T)\to\mathbb{R}
and
g:\Omega_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
are given measurable functions,
A:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(0,T)\to\mathbb{R}
is a given kernel coefficient satisfying

(1.2)

L^{-1}\leq A(x,y,t)\leq L\quad\text{and}\quad A(x,y,t)=A(y,x,t)\quad\text{for }(x,y,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(0,T)

and for some constant
L\geq 1
, and
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(0,T)\to\mathbb{R}
is the unknown. In addition,
\Phi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is a measurable function satisfying
\Phi(0)=0
and

(1.3)

\begin{cases}(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\xi^{\prime}))(\xi-\xi^{\prime})\geq L^{-1}|\xi-\xi^{\prime}|^{2},\\
|\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\xi^{\prime})|\leq L|\xi-\xi^{\prime}|\quad\text{for any }\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}.\end{cases}

Nonlocal parabolic problems appear naturally in the physical world, e.g., as in anomalous diffusion processes from the areas of physics, finance, biology, ecology, geophysics, and many others.
Particularly, the nonlocal nonlinear operators of the above types find their application in image processing
[
27
]
and phase transition models
[
25
]
.

It is known that when the leading operator appearing in (
1.1
) is linear and the right-hand side is regular enough, then solutions enjoy higher Hölder regularity, which in turn, yields improved Sobolev regularity. These assertions can be justified by the functional analysis tools along with a precise integral representation of the solution through suitable heat kernel type estimates. Unfortunately, when the operator is nonlinear, the aforementioned techniques fail to apply. To this end, our objective is to obtain a fine fractional Sobolev regularity for weak solutions to (
1.1
) by using purely analytic and geometric techniques. In particular, we introduce a unified approach of covering arguments to obtain some uniform measure density estimates for some level sets involving the solution, which we will explain in the sequel.

More precisely, our aim is to establish Calderón-Zygmund type estimates for weak solutions to (
1.1
) under a minimal regularity requirement on the kernel coefficient
A=A(x,y,t)
. More precisely, we want to find an extra condition on
A
besides (
1.2
) under which the following implication holds

\displaystyle f(x,t)-f(y,t)\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)\text{ with }f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}(0,T;L^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994ptg\in L^{\frac{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}{n+4s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{T})

(1.4)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\frac{u(x,t)-u(y,t)}{|x-y|^{s}}\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)

for any
q\in(2,\infty)
and
\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\min\left\{s-\frac{2s}{q},1-s\right\}\right)
with the desired Calderón-Zygmund type estimates like (
1.9
).
In particular, using the notion of fractional gradients introduced in
[
39
,
13
]
; that is,

d_{\beta}u(x,y,t):=\frac{u(x,t)-u(y,t)}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\quad\text{for any }\beta\in[0,1),

the implication (
1.1
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle d_{0}f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)\text{ with }f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}(0,T;L^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994ptg\in L^{\frac{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}{n+4s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{T})

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right).

1.2.
Some known results

For the elliptic problems, a self-improving property of a weak solution to the problem:

(1.5)

\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}u=(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f+g

is obtained by Kuusi, Mingione and Sire
[
35
]
by introducing the notion of dual pairs. When
\Phi(\xi)=\xi
and
A=A(x,y)
is Hölder continuous, Calderón-Zygmund type estimate for (
1.5
) is established by Mengesha, Schikorra and Yeepo
[
40
]
via commutator estimates. In addition, the aforementioned articles deal with more general equations as source terms involve
\tilde{s}
-fractional Laplacian with
\tilde{s}\neq\frac{s}{2}
. For operators with possibly discontinuous coefficients, such as VMO coefficients, Nowak
[
43
,
42
]
obtain Calderón-Zygmund type estimates when
f=0
by using the maximal function and the notion of dual pairs. We refer to
[
1
]
for the global Calderón-Zygmund type estimate of (
1.5
) with
A=1
,
\Phi(\xi)=\xi
,
f=0
and the zero Dirichlet condition on the exterior of the domain. The main tool employed in this work is the Green function representation of the solution.

We now mention some related results for the case of nonlinear nonlocal operators. When
\Phi(\xi)=|\xi|^{p-2}\xi
with
p>2
and
f=0
, Nowak and Diening
[
19
]
obtain sharp regularity results containing borderline cases by establishing precise pointwise bounds in terms of fractional sharp maximal functions. On the other hand, Calderón-Zygmund type estimates of solutions to the problem:

\mathcal{L}_{A}^{\Phi}u=(-\Delta_{p})^{\frac{s}{p}}f,

are established in
[
13
]
via a maximal function free technique which was first introduced in
[
2
]
.
We mention the work
[
22
]
as well for
L^{p}
-theory of a strong solution to nonlocal elliptic equations. For additional regularity results related to nonlocal elliptic equations, we refer to
[
34
,
23
,
46
,
9
,
8
,
18
,
37
,
17
,
45
,
44
,
15
,
14
,
12
,
24
,
5
,
32
,
41
,
36
]
and references therein.

For the parabolic problems, Auscher, Bortz, Egert and Saari
[
4
]
prove a self-improving property of solutions to (
1.1
) with
f=0
by using functional analysis techniques. When
\Phi(\xi)=\xi
,
A(x,y,t)\equiv 1
and
f=0
, Biccari, Warma and Zuazua
[
6
]
provide optimal regularity results of a weak solution by using a cut off argument. For the
L^{p}
-theory of strong solutions to nonlocal parabolic equations, we refer to
[
21
,
51
]
. We further mention
[
16
,
10
,
48
,
30
,
38
,
3
,
50
,
31
,
49
,
26
,
29
]
and references therein for various regularity results of nonlocal parabolic equations.

1.3.
Main results

To explain the desired Carderón-Zygmund type estimate (
1.1
),
we first introduce the notion of dual pairs. For a measurable function
F:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(0,T)
and
\tau\in(0,1)
, we define

D^{\tau}F(x,y,t)\coloneqq\frac{F(x,y,t)}{|x-y|^{\tau}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n-2\tau}},\quad\text{for any measurable set }\mathcal{A}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Furthermore, we write
\,d\mu_{\tau,t}=\,d\mu_{\tau}\,dt
. Then we observe that

(1.6)

d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)\Longleftrightarrow D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right),\,\mbox{where $\tau=\frac{q\sigma}{q-2}$.}

From this observation, we deduce that the solution
u
improves its integrability order as well as the differentiability order by achieving the same integrability as that of the associated non-homogeneous term in the Sobolev scale and a substantial gain in the differentiability order.

We now introduce a nonlocal tail space. We say that
u\in L^{p}(0,T;L^{1}_{2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
if

\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u(x,\cdot)|}{(1+|x|)^{n+2s}}\,dx\right\|_{L^{p}(0,T)}<\infty

for any
p\in[1,\infty]
. In particular, we write

\Tail_{p,2s}\left(u;B_{r}(x_{0})\times I\right)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{I}\left(r^{2s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(y,t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2s}}\,dy\right)^{p}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\quad\text{if }p\in[1,\infty)

and

\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(u;B_{r}(x_{0})\times I\right)=\sup_{t\in I}\left(r^{2s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(y,t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2s}}\,dy\right),

where
I\subset\mathbb{R}
is a bounded time interval.
We note from Hölder’s inequality that for any
1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty
,

(1.7)

\Tail_{p_{1},2s}\left(u;B_{r}(x_{0})\times I\right)\leq\Tail_{p_{2},2s}\left(u;B_{r}(x_{0})\times I\right).

As usual, a solution to (
1.1
) is defined in the weak sense as below.

Definition 1.1
.

Let
f\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;L^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)
with
d_{0}f\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)
and
g\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}}(\Omega_{T})
. We say that

u\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}\left(0,T;W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{s,2}(\Omega)\right)\cap C_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\Omega)\right)\cap L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}\left(0,T;L^{1}_{2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)

is a weak solution to (
1.1
) if

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Omega}-u\phi_{t}\,dx\,dt+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Phi\left(\frac{u(x,t)-u(y,t)}{|x-y|^{s}}\right)(\phi(x,t)-\phi(y,t))\frac{A(x,y,t)}{|x-y|^{n+s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}(u\phi)(x,t)\,dx\Bigg\rvert_{t=t_{1}}^{t=t_{2}}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(f(x,t)-f(y,t))(\phi(x,t)-\phi(y,t))\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n+s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Omega}g\phi\,dx\,dt

holds for any
\phi\in L^{2}(t_{1},t_{2};W^{s,2}(\Omega))\cap W^{1,2}\left(t_{1},t_{2};L^{2}(\Omega)\right)
with compact spatial support contained in
\Omega
and
(t_{1},t_{2})\Subset(0,T)
.

We next introduce the notion of
(\delta,R)
-vanishing condition on
A
, for some
\delta
and
R>0
. We say that
A
is
(\delta,R)
-vanishing in
\Omega_{T}
, if

\displaystyle\sup_{0<r\leq R,\,Q_{r}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|A(x,y,t)-(A)_{r,x_{0}}(t)|dx\,dy\,dt\leq\delta,

where
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})
and

(1.8)

(A)_{r,x_{0}}(t)\coloneqq\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}A(x,y,t)\,dx\,dy.

We now observe the following scaling invariance property for the problem (
1.1
).

Lemma 1.2
.

Let
Q_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
. Suppose that
A
is
(\delta,R)
-vanishing in
\Omega_{T}
. Then

\tilde{u}(x,t)=\frac{u(rx+x_{0},r^{2s}t+t_{0})}{r^{s}}

is a weak solution to

\tilde{u}_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{u}=(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}\tilde{f}+\tilde{g}\quad\text{ in }Q_{1},

where

\tilde{f}(x,t)=f(rx+x_{0},r^{2s}t+t_{0}),\quad\tilde{g}(x,t)=r^{s}g(rx+x_{0},r^{2s}t+t_{0})

and
\tilde{A}(x,y,t)=A(rx+x_{0},ry+x_{0},r^{2s}t+t_{0})
is
\left(\delta,\frac{R}{r}\right)
-vanishing in
Q_{1}
.

We now introduce our main results.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to problem (
1.1
). Let
R>0
and
q\in(2,\infty)
be given, and
fix
\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\min\left\{s-\frac{2s}{q},1-s\right\}\right)
. Then there is a constant
\delta=\delta(n,s,L,q,\sigma)\in(0,1)
such that if
A
is
(\delta,R)
-vanishing in
\Omega_{T}
,
f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;L^{1}_{s}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\right)
with
d_{0}f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\,;\,\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\right)
and
g\in L^{\frac{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}{n+4s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{T})
, then
d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\,;\,\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\right)
. Moreover, there is a constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\sigma)
such that

(1.9)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\frac{r}{2}}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(x_{0})}\left|d_{s+\sigma}u\right|^{q}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\left|\frac{d_{s}u}{r^{\sigma}}\right|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{s+\sigma}}\,;\,Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)

\displaystyle+c\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{r^{2s+2\sigma}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}\left(r^{{s-\sigma}}|g|\right)^{\frac{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}{n+4s}}\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{n+4s}{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\left|{d_{\sigma}f}\right|^{q}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+c\Tail_{q,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{\sigma}}\,;\,Q_{r}(z_{0})\right),

whenever
Q_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
and
r\in(0,R]
.

Remark 1
.

A few comments are in order for the restricted range of
\sigma
in Theorem
1.1
. In the elliptic case, we observe that a similar type of result holds for all
\sigma\in\left(0,\min\left\{s,1-s\right\}\right)
(see
[
13
, Theorem 1.2]
). However, in our case, to handle the nonlocal parabolic tail induced by the non-homogeneous term
f
, we have to impose the condition
\tau\in\left(0,s-\frac{2s}{q}\right)
(see (
5.27
) below). Therefore, from the observation (
1.6
), we deduce that the Calderón-Zygmund type estimate (
1.9
) holds under
\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\min\left\{s-\frac{2s}{q},1-s\right\}\right)
. In this regard, if
f=0
, then this restriction is removed and the results hold for a broader range of
\sigma
(see Theorem
1.2
below).

Remark 2
.

As we pointed out earlier, in the elliptic case, from regularity results for (
1.5
) with
g=0
, we deduce a higher regularity of a weak solution
u
to (
1.5
). However, in the parabolic case, it does not hold. More specifically, if
g\in L^{\tilde{q}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{T})
for some
\tilde{q}>2
, then we find a solution
f\in L^{\tilde{q}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;H^{s,\tilde{q}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)\right)\cap L^{\tilde{q}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(0,T;L^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
to

(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f(\cdot,t)=g(\cdot,t)\quad\text{in }\Omega

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
(see
[
13
, Subsection 1.2]
). This implies that
u
is a weak solution to

u_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{A}^{\Phi}u=(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f.

By Theorem
1.1
, we deduce that

u\in L^{\tilde{q}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{s+\sigma,\tilde{q}}(\Omega)\right)\text{ for any }\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}\right)\min\left\{s-\frac{2s}{\tilde{q}},1-s\right\}\right).

We now select
\tilde{q}=\frac{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}{n+4s}
. Since
\tilde{q}<q
, we do not obtain the desired result given in Theorem
1.2
. Therefore, we consider a more general non-homogeneous term which consists of
(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f
and
g
.

On account of Remark
1
, we obtain an improved Sobolev regularity when we consider only non-divergence data
g
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to problem (
1.1
) with
f=0
. Let
R>0
and
q\in(2,\infty)
be given, and
fix
\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\min\left\{s,1-s\right\}\right)
. Then there is a constant
\delta=\delta(n,s,L,q,\sigma)\in(0,1)
such that if
A
is
(\delta,R)
-vanishing in
\Omega_{T}
and
g\in L^{\frac{q\left(n+2s+\frac{2\sigma q}{q-2}\right)}{n+4s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{T})
, then
u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;W^{s+\sigma,q}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)\right)
with the estimate (
1.9
).

Remark 3
.

Let us compare the local Calderón-Zygmund theory and the nonlocal ones.
It is known that if
v
is a weak solution to

v_{t}-\mathrm{div\,}(BDv)=g,

then we obtain the following implication

g\in L^{\frac{q(n+2)}{n+4}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{T})\Longrightarrow v\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}(0,T;W^{1,q}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)),

whenever the coefficient
B
is
\delta
-vanishing for sufficiently small
\delta
depending only on the given data (see, for instance,
[
7
]
). We observe that in the limiting case when
\sigma\to 0
and
s\to 1
, the result in Theorem
1.2
is the same as the local one. However, in the nonlocal case, although the kernel coefficient is discontinuous, Theorem
1.2
implies that the solution
u
obtains not only higher integrability but also higher differentiability along the Sobolev-scale. This is in some sense a purely nonlocal phenomenon, since in order to observe such results in the local case, the coefficient
B
is assumed to have some fractional regularity in the literature (see
[
33
]
). A similar phenomenon for the nonlocal equations is observed in
[
43
,
42
,
35
,
4
]
and references therein.

1.4.
Working methods and novelties

We now briefly explain our approach to obtain the desired estimates (
A.15
) and (
1.9
). As usual, keeping the relation (
1.6
) in mind, to prove that the fractional gradient term
d_{s}u
is in
L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)
, it suffices to show that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{q-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|(x,y,t)>\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda<\infty

holds for any
\mathcal{Q}=B\times B\times\Lambda
, where
B\Subset\Omega
is a ball and
\Lambda\Subset(0,T)
is a time interval. To do this, we construct coverings for upper level sets of
|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|
inspired by the maximal function free technique as introduced in
[
2
]
.
Using an exit-time argument, we are able to construct coverings for the diagonal part of the upper level sets. For the off-diagonal part, we use a reverse Hölder-type inequality (see (
5.49
), below) which is obtained regardless of the information that
u
solves (
1.1
). As in
[
35
, Lemma 5.3]
, this inequality contains additional correction terms involving diagonal cylinders which induce some serious difficulties, as such cylinders do not come from any exit-time argument. We would like to mention that in the elliptic case, Calderón-Zygmund cube decomposition and an involved combinatorial argument are used to overcome these difficulties (see
[
35
]
). However, in the parabolic case, there are additional difficulties, since the correction terms contain
L^{2}
-oscillation integrals by sup norm term (see the second term of the right-hand side in (
5.32
) and Lemma
5.2
below). To this end, we employ Vitali’s covering lemma along with an exit-time argument instead of Calderón-Zygmund cube decomposition in order to construct coverings for upper-level sets of
|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|
. We would like to mention that this argument is new even in the elliptic case, and we believe that this argument can be applied to degenerate or singular nonlocal parabolic equations. We also point out that due to the appearance of the additional
L^{2}
-oscillation integrals by sup norm term, functionals used to apply an exit-time argument also contain a term of a similar kind which is usually not observed in the local parabolic problems (see (
5.11
)). We will elaborate on how to take care of this term while obtaining a good bound on the measure of exit-time cylinders in Remarks
6
and
7
below.
Moreover, we use a non-trivial exit time radius in the covering arguments in light of the rigorous tail estimates as in (
5.25
) and (
5.26
), since the additional
L^{2}
-oscillation terms by sup norm are estimated by the sum of
L^{2}
-integral of
d_{s}u
and
d_{0}f
,
L^{\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}}
-integral of
g
and tail terms of
u
and
f
(see Lemma
3.3
).
Consequently, by constructing suitable coverings, we are able to make use of comparison estimates, which further require some higher Hölder continuity estimates along with a self-improving property for limiting equations, and a boot strap argument to finally obtain the desired result (see Section
6
).

We would like to remark that a similar covering argument along with Gerhing’s Lemma (in the spirit of
[
35
]
) can be used to obtain a self-improving property of weak solutions to (
1.1
) without imposing any regularity assumption on the kernel coefficient
A
. Indeed, for the sake of completion, we prove a self-improving property of weak solutions to (
1.1
) with
f=g=0
(see Appendix
A
).
In addition, this result generalizes the ones given in
[
4
]
by allowing nonlinear structure assumptions on the nonlocal operator.

1.5.
Plan of the paper

This paper is organized as follows. In Section 2, we introduce some notations, embedding inequalities, properties of the measure
\mu_{\tau,t}
, tail estimates. In Section 3, we derive some energy estimates. Section 4 is devoted to establishing some comparison estimates. In Section 5, we construct coverings of upper level sets of fractional gradients for weak solutions. Section 6 contains the proof of the main theorem. We end the paper with two appendices. In the first appendix, we give the proof of a self-improving property for weak solutions to (
1.1
) with
f=g=0
, whereas the second appendix deals with the existence of a weak solution to the corresponding boundary value problem of (
1.1
).

2.
Preliminaries and Notations

As usual, we write
c
to mean a general constant equal to or bigger than 1 and it possibly changes from line to line. In addition, we employ parentheses to denote the relevant dependencies on parameters such as
c=c(n,s)
, and we denote

\mathsf{data}=\mathsf{data}(n,s,L,q,\sigma).

For
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, by the notation
a\approx_{n,s}b
, we mean that there is a constant
c=c(n,s)
such that
\frac{b}{c}\leq a\leq cb
.
A generic point
z\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
will be denoted by

z=(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}.

We write parameters
i,j,l,k
and
m
to mean nonnegative integers.
We denote a time interval as

\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})\coloneqq(t_{0}-r^{2s},t_{0}+r^{2s}).

The parabolic cylinder is defined by

Q_{r}(z_{0})=B_{r}(x_{0})\times\Lambda_{r}(t_{0}),

where
B_{r}(x_{0})
denotes the ball in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
centered at
x_{0}
with radius
r
. We write

\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0},y_{0})=B_{r}(x_{0})\times B_{r}(y_{0}),\quad\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})=B_{r}(x_{0})\times B_{r}(x_{0})

and

\mathcal{Q}_{r}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})=\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0},y_{0})\times\Lambda_{r}(t_{0}),\quad\mathcal{Q}_{r}(x_{0},t_{0})=\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})\times\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})

for any
x_{0},y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
r>0
.
For a given measurable function
h:\Omega_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
, we write for any
Q_{r}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
,

(h)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}h(x,t)\,dx\quad\text{and}\quad(h)_{Q_{r}(x_{0})}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}h(z)\,dz.

We denote the parabolic Sobolev conjugate of
p\in[1,\infty)
by

(2.1)

p_{\#}=p\left(1+\frac{2s}{n}\right).

We are going to mention some lemmas starting with the following embedding result.

Lemma 2.1
.

(see
[
20
, Lemma 2.3]
)

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
and
h\in L^{p}(0,T;W^{s,p}(B_{r}))\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(B_{r}))
. Then there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}|h|^{p_{\#}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\left(r^{sp}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{r}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}\frac{|h(x,t)-h(y,t)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{n+sp}}\,dx\,dy\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}|h|^{p}\,dz\right)\times\left(\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}|h|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{sp}{n}}.

In particular, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}|h-(h)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{p_{\#}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\left(r^{sp}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{r}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}\frac{|h(x,t)-h(y,t)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{n+sp}}\,dx\,dy\,dt\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}}|h-(h)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{sp}{n}}.

Next, we list some properties of the measure
\mu_{\tau,t}
.

Lemma 2.2
.

There exists a constant
C_{n}
depending only on
n
such that

(1)

For any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
R>0
, the following holds

(2.2)

\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{R}(x_{0},t_{0})\right)=C_{n}\frac{R^{n+2s+2\tau}}{\tau}.

(2)

Let
\rho
and
R
be any positive numbers, and let
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

(2.3)

\frac{\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{R}(x_{0},t_{0})\right)}{\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(x_{0},t_{0})\right)}=\left(\frac{R}{\rho}\right)^{n+2s+2\tau}.

(3)

Let
\mathcal{K}_{r}(x_{0},y_{0})
be any cube in
\mathcal{B}_{R}
for
r,R>0
and
x_{0},y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

(2.4)

\frac{\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{R}\right)}{\mu_{\tau,t}\big(\mathcal{K}_{r}(x_{0},y_{0})\times\Lambda_{r}\big)}\leq 2^{n}\frac{C_{n}}{\tau}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{2n+2s}.

(4)

Let
a\geq 1
. Then we have

(2.5)

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{aR}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\right)\leq{ca^{2n+2s}}{\tau}^{-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{R}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\right)

for some constant
c=c(n)
.

Proof.

Since the proofs of (1)-(3) follow from
[
13
, Lemma 2.1]
, we only give the proof of (4). If
x_{0}=y_{0}
, (4) follows from (2). We assume
x_{0}\neq y_{0}
. Let

\displaystyle D=\mathrm{diam}(B_{aR}(x_{0}),B_{aR}(y_{0})).

If
D\leq 4a
, then
\mathcal{Q}_{aR}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\subset\mathcal{Q}_{10aR}(x_{0},t_{0})
. Thus by (3), we get

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{aR}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\right)\leq\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{10aR}(x_{0},t_{0})\right)\leq\frac{ca^{2n+2s}}{\tau}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{R}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\right)

for some constant
c=c(n)
. If
D>4a
, we observe

\displaystyle D\leq|x-y|\leq 3D\quad\text{for any }x\in B_{aR}(x_{0})\text{ and }y\in B_{aR}(y_{0}).

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{aR}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\right)\leq\frac{(aR)^{2n+2s}}{D^{n-2\tau}}\leq ca^{2n+2s}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{R}(x_{0},y_{0},t_{0})\right)

for some constant
c=c(n)
. This completes the proof.
∎

We now give some useful estimates to control the parabolic tail.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
h\in L^{p}\left(\Lambda_{2};L^{1}_{2\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)
and
D^{\tau}d_{\tilde{s}}h\in L^{p}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{2};\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)
where
\beta\in(0,1)
,
\tilde{s}\in[0,\beta]
and
p\in[1,\infty)
. Let
Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\Subset Q_{2}
, where
z_{0}\in Q_{r_{1}}
with
0<r_{1}<2
. Suppose that there is a natural number
l\geq 1
such that

Q_{2^{l}\rho}(z_{0})\Subset Q_{2}.

Then for any integer
k\in[0,l]
, there are constants
c_{p}=c(n,p)
and
\tilde{c}=\tilde{c}(n)
independent of
k
and
l
such that

(2.6)

\displaystyle\Tail_{p,2\beta}\left(\frac{h-(h)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{\rho^{\tilde{s}+\tau}};Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\mathbb{A}_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}2^{i\left(-2\beta+\tilde{s}+\tau+\frac{2s}{p}\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2^{i}\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{\tilde{s}}h|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c_{p}\mathbb{A}_{k+1-l}\frac{2^{-2\beta l+\tilde{s}+\tau+\frac{2s}{p}}}{\rho^{\tilde{s}+\tau+\frac{2s}{p}}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\tilde{s}}h|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{c}\mathbb{A}_{l-k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{l}2^{j(-2\beta+\tilde{s}+\tau)}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2^{j}\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2^{j}\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{2^{j}\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|}{(2^{j}\rho)^{\tilde{s}+\tau}}\,dx

\displaystyle+\tilde{c}\mathbb{A}_{l-k}\frac{2^{-2\beta l+\tilde{s}+\tau}}{\rho^{\tilde{s}+\tau}}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{2}}(t)|}{2^{s+\tau}}\,dx+\frac{\tilde{c}}{(2-r_{1})^{n+2s}}\left(\frac{2}{\rho}\right)^{-2\beta+\tilde{s}+\tau-\frac{2s}{p}}\Tail_{p,2\beta}\left(\frac{h-(h)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{\tilde{s}+\tau}};Q_{2}\right),

where

\mathbb{A}_{m}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }m=1,2,\ldots,\\
0&\text{if }m=0,-1,\ldots.\end{cases}

Proof.

Using Minkowski’s inequality, we get that

\displaystyle\Tail_{p,2\beta}\left(\frac{h-(h)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{\rho^{\tilde{s}+\tau}};Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{l}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\left(\int_{B_{2^{i}\rho}(x_{0})\setminus B_{2^{i-1}\rho}(x_{0})}\rho^{2\beta-\tilde{s}-\tau}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2\beta}}\,dy\right)^{p}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\left(\int_{B_{2}\setminus B_{2^{l}\rho}(x_{0})}\rho^{2\beta-\tilde{s}-\tau}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2\beta}}\,dy\right)^{p}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2}}\rho^{2\beta-\tilde{s}-\tau}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2\beta}}\,dy\right)^{p}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\eqqcolon\sum_{i=1}^{l}I_{i}+\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}.

From the estimate of
T_{k}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
in
[
13
, Lemma 2.6]
, we have

\displaystyle I_{i}\leq\frac{2^{-2\beta i}}{\rho^{\tilde{s}+\tau}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2^{j}\rho}(x_{0})}|h-(h)_{B_{2^{j}\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|\,dy\right)^{p}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\eqqcolon 2^{-2\beta i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}I_{i,j}.

Using Hölder’s inequality and then following the same line as for (2.12) in
[
13
, Lemma 2.6]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{i}I_{i,j}

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{A}_{k}2^{j\left(\tilde{s}+\tau+\frac{2s}{p}\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2^{j}\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{\tilde{s}}h|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=k+1}^{i}\mathbb{A}_{i-k}2^{j(\tilde{s}+\tau)}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2^{j}\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{2^{j}\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|}{(2^{j}\rho)^{\tilde{s}+\tau}}\,dx,

for some constant
c_{p}=2^{n+2p}
, where we have taken supremum for the time variable if
j\geq k+1
.
Similarly, we estimate
\mathcal{T}_{1}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-2\beta l}\sum_{i=1}^{l}I_{l,i}+c_{p}\mathbb{A}_{k+1-l}2^{-2\beta l}\left(\frac{2}{\rho}\right)^{\tilde{s}+\tau+\frac{2s}{p}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\tilde{s}}h|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+c\mathbb{A}_{l-k}2^{-2\beta l}\left(\frac{2}{\rho}\right)^{\tilde{s}+\tau}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{2}}(t)|}{2^{\tilde{s}+\tau}}\,dx\eqqcolon\mathcal{T}_{1,1},

where
c=c(n)
.
Lastly, we estimate
\mathcal{T}_{2}
as

(2.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{T}_{1,1}+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\rho}\right)^{-2\beta+\tilde{s}+\tau}\left(\frac{2}{2-r_{1}}\right)^{n+2\beta}2^{-2\beta+\tilde{s}+\tau}\frac{|h-(h)_{B_{2}}(t)|}{|y|^{n+2\beta}}\,dy\right)^{p}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

where for the last term we have used the fact that

|y-x_{0}|\geq|y|-|x_{0}|\geq\frac{|y|(2-r_{1})}{2}\quad\text{for any }y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2}.

We combine all the estimates
I_{i}
,
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
and use Fubini’s theorem as in
[
13
, Lemma 2.6]
to get the desired result (
2.6
).
∎

Remark 4
.

By tracking the choice of the constant
c_{p}
appearing in Lemma
2.3
, we find that
c_{p}\leq c_{q}
if
p\leq q
.

We end this section with the following iteration lemma.

Lemma 2.4
.

(See
[
28
, Lemma 6.1]
)

Let
\phi:[1,2]\to\mathbb{R}
be a nonnegative bounded function. For
1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2
, we assume that

\phi(r_{1})\leq\frac{1}{2}\phi(r_{2})+\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\left(r_{2}-r_{1}\right)^{\frac{5n}{s}}},

where
\lambda_{0}>0
. Then,

\phi(1)\leq c\lambda_{0}

for some constant
c=c(n,s)
.

3.
Energy estimates and the Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities

In this section, we give energy estimates and derive Sobolev-Poincaré type inequalities from the energy estimates.
We first give an energy inequality of a weak solution
u
to (
1.1
).

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
u
be a local weak solution to (
1.1
). Let
0<\rho<r\leq 2\rho
with
Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
. Then, there is a constant
c=c(n,s,L)
such that

(3.1)

\displaystyle\left[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt+\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x,t)-k|^{2}}{\rho^{2s}}\,dx\right]

\displaystyle\leq c\frac{r^{n+2-2s}}{(r-\rho)^{n+2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|u-k|^{2}\,dz+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{2(n+2s)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}\left(r^{s}|g|\right)^{\gamma}\,dz\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{2(n+2s)}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{s}};Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{2(n+2s)}\Tail_{2,s}\left(f-(f)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t);Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)^{2},

where
k\in\mathbb{R}
and

(3.2)

\gamma=\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}.

Proof.

Since
u-k
is also a weak solution to (
1.1
), we may assume that
k=0
. Let us take a cutoff function
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(B_{\frac{\rho+r}{2}}\right)
such that

\psi\equiv 1\text{ on }B_{\rho}(x_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad|D\psi|\leq\frac{16}{r-\rho},

and a smooth function
\eta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that

\eta\equiv 1\text{ on }\left(t_{0}-\left(\frac{r^{2s}+\rho^{2s}}{2}\right),\infty\right),\quad\eta\equiv 0\text{ on }\left(-\infty,t_{0}-\left(\frac{3r^{2s}+\rho^{2s}}{4}\right)\right]\quad\text{and}\quad|\eta^{\prime}|\leq\frac{16}{r^{2s}-\rho^{2s}}.

With the aid of
[
11
, Lemma 3.2]
, we deduce that

(3.3)

\displaystyle I:

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|(u\psi\eta)(x,t)-(u\psi\eta)(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt+\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{r}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|(u\psi\eta)(x,t)|^{2}}{r^{2s}}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\frac{r^{2(1-s)}}{(r-\rho)^{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|u|^{2}\,dz+c\frac{1}{r^{2s}-\rho^{2s}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|u|^{2}\,dz+c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|g||u\psi\eta|\,dz

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{n+2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(y,t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2s}}\,dy\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|(u\psi\eta)(x,t)|\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle+c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{(f(x,t)-f(y,t))\left((u\psi\eta)(x,t)-(u\psi\eta)(y,t)\right)}{|x-y|^{n+s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{n+s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+s}}\,dy\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|(u\psi\eta)(x,t)|\,dx\,dt\eqqcolon\sum_{i=1}^{6}I_{i}.

After a few simple algebraic computations along with the fact that
\rho<r\leq 2\rho
, we observe that
I_{2}\leq cI_{1}
. Using Hölder’s inequality, Lemma
2.1
and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}(r^{s}|g|)^{\gamma}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u\psi\eta}{r^{s}}\right|^{2_{\#}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2_{\#}}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}(r^{s}|g|)^{\gamma}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+\frac{I}{8}+r^{-2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|u|^{2}\,dx\,dt

and

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{(n+2s)}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{s}};Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u\psi\eta}{r^{s}}\right|^{2_{\#}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2_{\#}}}+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{n}I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{2(n+2s)}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{s}};Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)^{2}+\frac{I}{8}+r^{-2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|u|^{2}\,dx\,dt+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{n}I_{1},

where the constant
2_{\#}
is defined in (
2.1
).
On the other hand, we obtain

I_{5}\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n}}\,dx\,dy\,dt+\frac{I}{8}

by Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality. For the last two terms, we first observe that

\displaystyle I_{5}+I_{6}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{n+s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{r}(x_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-(f)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+s}}|(u\psi\eta)(x,t)|\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{n+s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(y,t)-(f)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+s}}\,dy\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|(u\psi\eta)(x,t)|\,dx\,dt.

By following the same line as in the estimate of
I_{2,2}
in
[
13
, Lemma 3.3]
, we estimate
I_{5}
as

\displaystyle I_{5}+I_{6}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{I}{8}+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{2(n+2s)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+c\Tail_{2,s}\left(f-(f)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t);Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)^{2}.

Using the definitions of
\psi
and
\eta
, we estimate
I
as

\displaystyle I\geq\left[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt+\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x,t)|^{2}}{\rho^{2s}}\,dx\right].

We combine the estimates
I
and
I_{i}
for each
i=1,2,\ldots,6
along with (
3.3
) to obtain (
3.1
).
∎

Next we give a gluing lemma.

Lemma 3.2
.

(See
[
11
, Lemma 4.5]
.)

Let
Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
. Take
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(B_{\frac{3\rho}{4}}(x_{0})\right)
with
\psi\equiv 1
in
B_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(x_{0})
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\left|(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t_{1})-(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2s-1}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{n+2s-1}}\,dy\,dz+c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+2s}}\,dy\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\rho^{2s-1}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{n+s-1}}\,dy\,dz+c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+s}}\,dy\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}|g|\,dz

for some constant
c=c(n,s)
, where
(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t)=\frac{1}{\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}}\int_{B_{\rho}}u(x,t)\psi(x)\,dx
.

We now show that
L^{2}
-oscillation integral by the sup-norm is estimated by the sum of
L^{2}
-integral of
d_{s}u
and
d_{0}f
,
L^{\gamma}
-integral of
g
, and tail terms of
u
and
f
. Before giving the estimate, we define a function
G:\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
by

(3.4)

G(x,y,t)=g(x,t).

We directly deduce that

(3.5)

\int_{Q_{r}}|g|^{p}\,dz\approx_{n,s,p}\frac{1}{r^{2\tau}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|G|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
) and let
Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
. Then we have

(3.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\rho^{2s+2\tau}}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+c_{0}\Tail_{2,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{(2\rho)^{s+\tau}};Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+c_{0}\Tail_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{(2\rho)^{\tau}};Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+c_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}\left((2\rho)^{s-\tau}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}},

where
c_{0}=c_{0}(n,s,L)
is a constant.

Proof.

We may assume that
z_{0}=0
.
Using (
3.1
) with
r=2\rho
and
k=(u)_{Q_{\rho}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{|u(x,t)-(u)_{Q_{\rho}}|^{2}}{\rho^{2s+2\tau}}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{2\rho}}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{2\rho}}|^{2}}{(2\rho)^{2s+2\tau}}\,dz+\frac{c}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2\rho}}\left((2\rho)^{s-\tau}|g|\right)^{\gamma}\,dz\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+c\left[\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2\rho}}(t)}{(2\rho)^{s+\tau}};Q_{2\rho}\right)^{2}+\Tail_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2\rho}}(t)}{(2\rho)^{\tau}};Q_{2\rho}\right)^{2}\right].

Applying (
A.3
) and (
2.2
) to the first term and the third term in the right-hand side of the above inequality, respectively, we obtain the desired estimate (
3.6
).
∎

4.
Comparison estimates

This section is devoted to establishing comparison estimates. We now assume

(4.1)

\tau\in\left(0,\min\{s,1-s\}\right).

Before proving comparison estimates, we first give two lemmas. The first one is a self-improving property for weak solutions to the corresponding homogeneous problem of (
1.1
).

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
w\in L^{2}\left(\Lambda_{3};W^{s,2}(B_{3})\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\Lambda_{3};L^{1}_{2s}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\right)
be a weak solution to

w_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}w=0\quad\text{in }Q_{3}.

Then there are constants
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(n,s,L)\in(0,1)
and
c=c(n,s,L)
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{w-(w)_{B_{3}}(t)}{3^{s+\tau}};Q_{3}\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|w-(w)_{B_{3}}(t)|^{2}}{3^{2(s+\tau)}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

By Theorem
A.1
below, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left|r^{\epsilon_{1}}{d_{s+\epsilon_{1}}w}\right|^{2+\epsilon_{1}}\frac{\,dx\,dz}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2+\epsilon_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{B_{2r}}|d_{s}w|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dz}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)+c\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{w-(w)_{B_{2r}}(t)}{(2r)^{s}};Q_{2r}\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2r}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2r}}\frac{|w-(w)_{B_{2r}}(t)|^{2}}{(2r)^{2s}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

for some constant
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{1}(n,s,L)\in(0,1)
, where
Q_{2r}\subset Q_{3}
. By taking
\epsilon_{0}=\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{2}
and using (
2.2
), we get

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2r}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{w-(w)_{B_{2r}}(t)}{(2r)^{s+\tau}};Q_{2r}\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2r}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2r}}\frac{|w-(w)_{B_{2r}}(t)|^{2}}{(2r)^{2(s+\tau)}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The standard covering argument along with Lemma
5.2
gives the desired result (see
[
13
, Lemma 3.1]
for more details).
∎

The second one is a higher Hölder regularity of weak solutions to fractional parabolic equations with locally constant coefficients with respect to the spatial variables.

Lemma 4.2
.

(See
[
11
, Theorem 1.2]
)
Let
v\in L^{2}\left(\Lambda_{2};W^{s,2}(B_{2})\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\Lambda_{2};L^{1}_{2s}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\right)
be a weak solution to

v_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A_{2}(t)}v=0\quad\text{in }Q_{2},

where we denote
A_{2}(t)=A_{2,0}(t)
which is defined in (
1.8
).
Then for any
\alpha\in(0,\min\left\{2s,1\right\})
, there is a constant
c=c(n,s,L,\alpha)
such that

[v]_{C^{\alpha,\frac{\alpha}{2s}}(Q_{1})}\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}v|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(v-(v)_{B_{2}}(t);Q_{2}\right).

We are now in position to prove the following comparison lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
Q_{4}\Subset\Omega_{T}
. For any
\epsilon>0
, there is a constant
\delta=\delta(n,s,L,\epsilon)
such that for any weak solution
u
to (
1.1
) satisfying

(4.2)

\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{4}}(t)}{4^{s+\tau}}\,;\,Q_{4}\right)^{2}\leq 1

and

(4.3)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}\left(4^{s-\tau}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+\Tail_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)}{4^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|A-(A)_{2}(t)|\,dx\,dy\,dt\right)^{2}\leq\delta^{2},

there is a solution
v
to

v_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A_{{2}}(t)}v=0\quad\text{in }Q_{2}

such that

(4.4)

\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-v)|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\leq\epsilon^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|D^{\tau}d_{s}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{1}\right)}\leq c,

where
c=c(n,s,L,\tau)
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into several steps for the ease of readability.

Step 1
:
First comparison estimates
. For a fixed weak solution
u
to problem (
1.1
), we consider the following problem:

(4.5)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}w_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}w&=0\quad\mbox{in }Q_{3},\\
w&=u\quad\mbox{in }(\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{3})\times\Lambda_{3}\cup B_{3}\times\{-3^{2s}\}.\end{array}\right.

We intend to apply Lemma
B.1
for the existence and uniqueness of
w
. To this end, it remains to show that
u_{t}\in L^{2}(\Lambda_{3};W^{s,2}(B_{4}))^{*}
. Indeed, using the fact that
u
is a solution to problem (
1.1
), we find that for all
\phi\in L^{2}(\Lambda_{3};W^{s,2}(B_{4}))\cap C^{1}_{0}(\Lambda_{3};L^{2}(B_{4}))
, there holds

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\Lambda_{3}}\langle u,\phi_{t}\rangle dt\bigg|\leq c\int_{\Lambda_{3}}\|\phi(\cdot,t)\|_{W^{s,2}(B_{4})}dt,

for some
c
depending only on
n,s,L,u,f
and
g
.
Thus, we have the existence of
w\in L^{2}\left(\Lambda_{3};W^{s,2}(B_{4})\right)\cap C\left(\Lambda_{3};L^{2}(B_{3})\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\Lambda_{3};L^{1}_{2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)
satisfying (
4.5
). Then
\varphi:=u-w
solves

\varphi_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{A}^{\Phi}u-\mathcal{L}_{A}^{\Phi}w=(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f+g\quad\text{in }Q_{3}.

With the help of an approximation argument, we take
\varphi
as a test function to the above equation to see that for every
\tilde{T}\in(-3^{2s},3^{2s}]
, setting
\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}:=(-3^{2s},\tilde{T}]
, there holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{3}}(\varphi(x,\tilde{T}))^{2}\,dx+\frac{1}{L}\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|^{2}\frac{A(x,y,t)}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|(f(x,t)-f(y,t))(\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t))|\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+s}}+\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}|g\varphi|\,dz,

where we have used the fact that
\varphi(\cdot,-3^{2s})=0
in
B_{3}
and the first condition in (
1.3
).
Noting the bounds on
A
, the above expression yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{3}}(\varphi(x,\tilde{T}))^{2}\,dx+\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq L^{2}\underbrace{\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{4}}\int_{B_{4}}|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|\,|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+s}}}_{=:J_{1}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle\quad+2L^{2}\underbrace{\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{4}}|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|\,|\varphi(x,t)|\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+s}}}_{=:J_{2}}+L^{2}\underbrace{\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}|g\varphi|\,dz}_{=:J_{3}}.

For
J_{1}
, applying Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, we observe that

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\Lambda_{3}}\int_{B_{4}}\int_{B_{4}}|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{4}}\int_{B_{4}}|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}d\mu_{\tau,t}+\frac{1}{4L^{2}}\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{4}}\int_{B_{4}}|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+2s}},

where
c=c(n,s,L)
. On a similar account, we deduce that

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{4}}\big(|f(x,t)-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)|+|f(y,t)-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)|\big)|\varphi(x,t)|\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+s}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{\Lambda_{4}}\int_{B_{4}}|f(x,t)-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)|^{2}dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}\varphi(x,t)^{2}dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\left(\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}|\varphi|^{2}\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Lambda_{3}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{4}}\frac{|f(y,t)-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)|}{|y|^{n+s}}dy\right)^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Noticing the fact that
\varphi(\cdot,t)=0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{3}
for a.e.
t\in\Lambda_{3}
,
we employ the Sobolev-Poincaré inequality to get

\displaystyle J_{2}\leq\frac{1}{8L^{2}}\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{4}}\int_{B_{4}}|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}+\frac{c}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}d\mu_{\tau,t}+c\,{\rm Tail}_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)}{4^{\tau}};Q_{4}\right)^{2},

where
c=c(n,s,L)
. Now for
J_{3}
, employing Hölder’s inequality, Lemma
2.1
and Young’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle J_{3}\leq c\left(\int_{Q_{3}}|g(x,t)|^{\gamma}dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+\frac{1}{4L^{2}}\int_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}\,dx\,dy\,dt+\frac{1}{4L^{2}}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\int_{B_{3}}|\varphi(x,t)|^{2}dx.

Therefore, using the estimates of
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
in (
4
), taking supremum over
\tilde{T}\in(-3^{2s},3^{2s}]
and recalling the definition of
G
from (
3.4
) along with (
3.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}(u-w)^{2}(x,t)\,dx+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-w)|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}\left(4^{s-\tau}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+\frac{c}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{4}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}d\mu_{\tau,t}+c\,{\rm Tail}_{2,s}\Big(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{4}}(t)}{4^{\tau}};Q_{4}\Big)^{2}\leq c\,\delta^{2},

where we have also used (
2.2
) and (
4.3
).

Step 2
:
Uniform self-improving inequality for
w
.
We first observe from Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{w-(w)_{B_{3}}(t)}{3^{s+\tau}};Q_{3}\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|w-(w)_{B_{3}}(t)|^{2}}{3^{2(s+\tau)}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(n,s,L)\in(0,1)
and
c=c(n,s,L)
. Furthermore, by using (
4
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-w)|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq c.

Using (
3.6
) with a slight modification, (
4
) and (
4.2
), we next have

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|w-(w)_{B_{3}}(t)|^{2}}{3^{2(s+\tau)}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|u-(u)_{B_{3}}(t)|^{2}}{3^{2(s+\tau)}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|u-w|^{2}}{3^{2(s+\tau)}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq c.

For the tail term, a simple computation together with (
4
) and (
4.2
) yields

\displaystyle\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{w-(w)_{B_{3}}(t)}{3^{s+\tau}};Q_{3}\right)\leq\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{3}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3}}\frac{|w-u|^{2}}{3^{2(\tau+s)}}\,dx+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{3}}(t)}{3^{s+\tau}};Q_{3}\right)\leq c.

Consequently,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}}\leq c.

Step 3
:
Second comparison estimates
. For
w
as in Step 1, we consider the following problem:

(4.9)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}v_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A_{2}(t)}v&=0\quad\mbox{in }Q_{2},\\
v&=w\quad\mbox{in }(\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2})\times\Lambda_{2}\cup B_{2}\times\{-2^{2s}\}.\end{array}\right.

Similar to Step 1, we have the existence of a unique solution
v\in L^{2}\left(\Lambda_{2};W^{s,2}(B_{3})\right)\cap C\left(\Lambda_{2};L^{2}(B_{2})\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\Lambda_{2};L^{1}_{2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)
to the problem (
4.9
). Taking
\tilde{\varphi}:=v-w
as a test function (upon approximation) to

\tilde{\varphi}_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A_{2}(t)}v-\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A_{2}(t)}w=\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}w-\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A_{2}(t)}w\quad\text{in }Q_{2}

and then using (
1.3
) and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{\varphi}|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\int_{B_{2}}|\tilde{\varphi}(x,t)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\Lambda_{2}}\int_{B_{2}}\int_{B_{2}}|A(x,t)-(A)_{2}(t)|\frac{|w(x,t)-w(y,t)||\tilde{\varphi}(x,t)-\tilde{\varphi}(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{n+2s}}dx\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{\varphi}|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}w|^{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{\Lambda_{2}}\int_{B_{2}}\int_{B_{2}}|A-(A)_{2}(t)|^{2\frac{1+\epsilon_{0}}{\epsilon_{0}}}dx\,dy\,dt\right)^{\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}}.

Finally, up on using the vanishing condition on
A
and (
4.8
), the above expression yields

(4.10)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}(v-w)|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\delta^{\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2(1+\epsilon_{0})}},

for some
c=c(n,s,L)
. Coupling (
4
) with (
4.10
