\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\right\}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{\widetilde{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j}\right\}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\in\mathcal{A}}

so that

\displaystyle\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y,t)|\geq\lambda\}\subset\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\cup\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\\
\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\in\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\widetilde{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j}),

which follows from the steps 2-3 along with (
5.22
), (
5.38
) and (
5.45
). In addition, using (
5.49
), (
5.65
), (
5.67
) and (
5.31
) along with the choice of the constants
a_{u}=\tilde{a}_{u}\kappa
,
a_{f}=\tilde{a}_{f}\kappa
and
a_{g}=\tilde{a}_{g}\kappa
given in (
5.31
), we get (
5.10
) and (
5.8
). This completes the proof.
∎

Before ending this section, we give some estimates which are useful in the context of the comparison Lemma
4.4
.

Remark 9
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})
be the cylinder chosen in Lemma
5.1
. Then we want to show that

(5.76)

\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(x_{i})}(t)}{(4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i})^{s+\tau}}\,;\,Q_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\right)^{2}\leq(c\lambda)^{2}

and

(5.77)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}\left((4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i})^{s-\tau}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}

\displaystyle+\Tail_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(x_{i})}(t)}{(4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i})^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\right)^{2}\leq(c\lambda\delta)^{2}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
. We first note from (
5.14
), (
5.20
) and (
5.23
) that there is a natural number
l
such that

5^{\frac{2}{s}}\times 2^{j_{0}+2}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}<2^{l}\times\overline{\rho}\leq 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\times 2^{j_{0}+3}\mathcal{R}_{1,2},

where we denote
\overline{\rho}=4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}
for a clear notation.
After a few modifications of the proof for (
2.6
) with
p=\infty
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}(t)}{\overline{\rho}^{s+\tau}};Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\sum_{j=1}^{l}2^{j(-s+\tau)}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2^{j}\overline{\rho}}(t_{i})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2^{j}\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}\frac{|u-(u)_{B_{2^{j}\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}(t)|}{(2^{j}\overline{\rho})^{s+\tau}}\,dx+\frac{2^{-2sl+s+\tau}}{\overline{\rho}^{s+\tau}}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)|}{2^{s+\tau}}\,dx\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{(2-r_{1})^{n+2s}}\left(\frac{2}{\overline{\rho}}\right)^{-s+\tau}\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{s+\tau}};Q_{2}\right),

where
c=c(n,s)
. Then using (
5.7
), (
5.20
) and the fact that

2^{-2sl}\left(\frac{2}{\overline{\rho}}\right)^{s+\tau}\leq\frac{c}{\mathcal{R}_{1,2}^{\frac{5n}{s}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}2^{i\left(-s+\tau\right)}\leq c(n,s,\tau),

we estimate the above term as

(5.78)

\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}(t)}{\overline{\rho}^{s+\tau}};Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})\right)\leq c\lambda,

where
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
. In addition, Hölder’s inequality and (
5.20
) imply

(5.79)

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq c\lambda.

We combine the above two estimates to obtain (
5.76
). Similarly, with the aid of (
1.7
) and (
2.6
) along with (
5.4
), we get that

\displaystyle\Tail_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}(t)}{(\overline{\rho})^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Tail_{\tilde{q},s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}(t)}{(\overline{\rho})^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{q}\sum_{j=1}^{l}2^{i\left(-s+\tau+\frac{2s}{\tilde{q}}\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2^{j}\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}+c_{q}2^{-sl}\left(\frac{2}{\overline{\rho}}\right)^{\tau+s}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\tilde{c}}{(2-r_{1})^{n+2s}}\left(\frac{2}{\overline{\rho}}\right)^{-s+\tau+\frac{2s}{\tilde{q}}}\Tail_{q,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{\tau}};Q_{2}\right).

Hence, as in (
5.78
) and (
5.79
), there is a constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
such that

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Tail_{2,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\overline{\rho}}(x_{i})}(t)}{(\overline{\rho})^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{\overline{\rho}}(z_{i})\right)\leq c\delta\lambda.

Additionally, (
5.20
) and (
5.36
) yield that

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}\left((4\times 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}\rho_{i})^{s-\tau}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\leq c\delta\lambda.

We combine the above two inequality to show that (
5.77
) holds.

6.
L^{q}
-estimate of
d_{s}u

In this section, we prove our main theorem. Since we have established comparison estimates and constructed coverings of upper level sets, we are able to obtain
L^{q}
-estimate of
d_{s}u
with the estimate (
1.9
) via a bootstrap argument as in
[
13
, Theorem 1.2]
.
Let us define

(6.1)

p_{h}=2\left(1+\frac{s}{n}\right)^{h},\quad\mbox{ for }h=0,1,2,\ldots.

Then there is a positive integer
h_{q}
such that

(6.2)

p_{h_{q}-1}<q\leq p_{h_{q}}.

We now prove the following lemma which is an essential ingredient to use a boot strap argument.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
h\in\{0,1,\ldots,h_{q}-1\}
. Suppose that
u
is a weak solution to (
5.1
) with

f\in L^{q}\left(\Lambda_{2};L^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}+|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}+|G|^{\frac{q\gamma}{b_{\tau}}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}<\infty.

Then there is a sufficiently small
\delta=\delta(n,s,L,q,\tau)\in(0,1]
independent of
h
such that if
A
is
(\delta,2)
-vanishing in
Q_{2}
, then we have that

(6.3)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{s+\tau}}\,;\,Q_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{2}}|^{2}}{2^{2s+2\tau}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\left(2^{{s-\tau}}|G|\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}\gamma}{b_{\tau}}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{b_{\tau}}{\tilde{p}\gamma}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\Tail_{q,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{2}\right)\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
, where the constant
b_{\tau}
is defined in (
5.9
) and

(6.4)

\tilde{p}=\begin{cases}p_{h+1}&\text{if }h<h_{q}-1,\\
q&\text{if }h=h_{q}-1.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let us first fix
1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2
and
\epsilon>0
. Then we select
\lambda_{0}
as given in Lemma
5.1
with
p=p_{h}
and select
\delta=\delta(n,s,L,\epsilon)
determined in Lemma
4.4
. For any
N\geq\lambda_{0}
, we define a function
\phi:[1,2]\to\mathbb{R}
by

(6.5)

\phi_{N}(r)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}},

where

|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}=\min\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|,N\right\}
. We now claim that for
N\geq\lambda_{0}
, the following holds

(6.6)

\phi_{N}(r_{1})\leq\frac{\phi_{N}(r_{2})}{2}+c\lambda_{0}+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\left(2^{{s-\tau}}|G|\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}\gamma}{b_{\tau}}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{b_{\tau}}{\tilde{p}\gamma}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
. Using Fubini’s theorem, we observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\tilde{p}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}}\tilde{p}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}}^{N}\tilde{p}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda\eqqcolon I_{1}+I_{2},

where
\mathcal{M}>1
is a constant which will be determined later and
N>\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}
. We now estimate
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
.

Estimate of
I_{1}
.

A simple calculation yields that

I_{1}\leq\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\right)(\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0})^{\tilde{p}}.

Estimate of
I_{2}
.

By a change of variable and (
5.6
) of Lemma
5.1
with
p=p_{h}
, we get that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\tilde{p}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\geq 0}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\tilde{p}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\tilde{p}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\eqqcolon I_{2,1}+I_{2,2},

where we have used the fact that

\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}(x,y,t)>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\subset\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|(x,y,t)>\lambda\right\}.

By assuming

(6.7)

\mathcal{M}>c_{c}c_{d},

where the constants
c_{c}=c_{c}(n,s,L,\tau)
and
c_{d}=c_{d}(n,s,L,q,\tau)
are given in Lemma
4.4
with
\rho_{i}
replaced by
5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}
and Remark
9
, respectively, we now estimate
I_{2,1}
as

(6.8)

\displaystyle I_{2,1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\tilde{p}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{\tilde{p}-1}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\,:\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-v)|_{N}(x,y,t)>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\tilde{p}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{\tilde{p}-3}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-v)|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{i}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\tilde{p}-2}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\epsilon^{2}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\right)\,d\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
. In the above estimates, we have used weak 1-1 estimates, (
4.11
) and (
2.3
). On the other hand, using weak 1-1 estimates, (
5.10
), (
2.1
) and (
6.1
), we have that

\displaystyle I_{2,2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{p}\lambda^{\tilde{p}}\sum_{j}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{-(p_{h})_{\#}+\tilde{p}-1}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{(p_{h})_{\#}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{p}(c_{od,h})^{(p_{h})_{\#}}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}}{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2s}{n}}}\sum_{j}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\right)\,d\lambda,

where the constant
c_{od,h}=c_{od,h}(n,s,p_{h},\tau)
is determined in (
5.10
). Note that the constant
c=\tilde{p}\times\max\limits_{h=0,1,\ldots,h_{q}-1}\left(c_{od,h}\right)^{q_{\#}}
depends only on
n,s,L,q
and
\tau
and is bigger than the constant
\tilde{p}(c_{od,h})^{(p_{h})_{\#}}
, as
h_{q}
depends only on
n,s,q
and
\tau
(see (
6.2
)). Hence, we have that

(6.9)

\displaystyle I_{2,2}\leq\frac{c\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}}{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2s}{n}}}\sum_{j}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mu_{\tau,t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\right)\,d\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,q,\tau)
.
Combine (
5.8
) with
p=p_{h}
, (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) to see that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\tilde{p}-3}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2s}{n}}}\right)\frac{c}{\lambda^{p_{h}}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|>a_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+c\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\tilde{p}-3}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2s}{n}}}\right)\frac{c}{(\delta\lambda)^{\tilde{q}}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|>a_{f}\delta\lambda\}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+c\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\lambda^{\tilde{p}-1}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\tilde{p}-3}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2s}{n}}}\right)\frac{c}{(\delta\lambda)^{b_{\tau}}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|G|^{\gamma}>(a_{g}\delta\lambda)^{b_{\tau}}G_{0}^{-1}\}}|G|^{\gamma}G_{0}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\,d\lambda\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}

where the constants
a_{u}
,
a_{f}
and
a_{g}
are determined in (
5.8
), and the constants
G_{0}
and
\tilde{q}
are defined in (
5.9
) and (
5.3
), respectively.
Using Fubini’s theorem and taking
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,s,L,q,\tau)>1
which satisfies (
6.7
) and then choosing
\epsilon=\epsilon(n,s,L,q,\tau)\in(0,1)
, we have that

J_{1}\leq\frac{1}{10^{5nq}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{N}^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}.

On the other hand, if
\tilde{p}\leq\tilde{q}
, then we estimate

(6.10)

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\lambda_{0}^{q-\tilde{p}}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\lambda^{q-1}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\tilde{p}-3}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2s}{n}}}\right)\frac{c}{(\delta\lambda)^{\tilde{q}}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|>a\lambda\}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\lambda_{0}^{q-\tilde{p}}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}}{q}},

where we have used
1\leq\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{0}}\right)^{q-\tilde{p}}
, Fubini’s theorem (thanks to the relation
\tilde{q}<q
, from (
5.5
)) and the fact that
\lambda_{0}^{\tilde{p}-q}\leq c\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}-q}{q}}
.
If
\tilde{p}>\tilde{q}
, Fubini’s theorem and Hölder’s inequality yield that

J_{2}\leq c\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\leq c\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}}{q}}.

Similarly, we get that

J_{3}\leq c\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}\left(|G|^{\gamma}G_{0}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}}{b_{\tau}}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\left(2^{{s-\tau}}|G|\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}\gamma}{b_{\tau}}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{b_{\tau}}{\gamma}}.

Consequently, combining all the estimates
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
and recalling (
2.2
) and (
6.5
), we obtain the desired result (
6.6
). We recall the definitions of
\lambda_{0}
given in (
5.7
) and
G_{0}
given in (
5.9
). Then using Lemma
2.4
and passing to the limit
N\to 0
, we get (
6.3
).
∎

With the aid of Lemma
6.1
, we now prove our main theorem.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.
Let us fix
Q_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
with
r\in(0,R]
. We now take

(6.11)

\tau=\frac{\sigma q}{q-2}<\min\left\{s-\frac{2s}{q},1-s\right\}.

Let us choose
\delta=\delta(n,s,L,q,\sigma)
determined in Lemma
6.1
. We claim that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{R}(u,f,g,p_{0},\tilde{p},Q_{r}(z_{0}))

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
, where
\tilde{p}
is given in (
6.4
) with
h=0
and we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(u,f,g,p_{0},\tilde{p},Q_{r}(z_{0}))=

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{s+\tau}}\,;\,Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{r^{2s+2\tau}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}\left(r^{\frac{s-\tau}{\tilde{p}}}|G|\right)^{\frac{\tilde{p}\gamma}{b_{\tau}}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{b_{\tau}}{\tilde{p}\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\Tail_{q,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{\tau}}\,;\,Q_{r}(z_{0})\right).

To this end, we define for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
t\in\Lambda_{2}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(x,t)=\frac{u\left(r_{s}x+x_{1},r_{s}^{2s}t+t_{1}\right)}{r_{s}^{s+\tau}},\hskip 9.24994pt\tilde{f}(x,t)=\frac{f\left(r_{s}x+x_{1},r_{s}^{2s}t+t_{1}\right)}{r_{s}^{\tau}},

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(x,t)=r_{s}^{s-\tau}g\left(r_{s}x+x_{1},r_{s}^{2s}t+t_{1}\right),\hskip 9.24994pt\tilde{A}(x,y,t)=A\left(r_{s}x+x_{1},r_{s}y+x_{1},r_{s}^{2s}t+t_{1}\right),\hskip 9.24994pt\tilde{\Phi}(\xi)=\frac{\Phi(r_{s}^{\tau}\xi)}{r_{s}^{\tau}}

where
z_{1}\in\overline{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})
and
r_{s}=\left(\frac{s(\sqrt{2}-1)}{4}\right)^{\frac{2}{s}}r
, in order to see that
\tilde{u}
is a weak solution to (
5.1
) with
f=\tilde{f}

g=\tilde{g}
,
A=\tilde{A}
and
\Phi=\tilde{\Phi}
. Moreover, we observe that

(6.13)

Q_{r_{s}}(z_{1})\subset Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0}).

We now apply Lemma
6.1
with
u=\tilde{u}
,
f=\tilde{f}
,
g=\tilde{g}
and

, and use change of the variables to get that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{2}}(z_{1})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{R}(u,f,g,p_{0},\tilde{p},Q_{r_{s}}(z_{1}))

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. After a few algebraic calculations along with Lemma
2.3
, (
2.2
) and (
6.13
), the expression in
(
6.14
) is estimated as

(6.15)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{2}}(z_{1})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{R}\left(u,f,g,p_{0},\tilde{p},Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0}))\right)

On the other hand, for any
\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}\equiv\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\times\Lambda_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}(t_{2})
with
(x_{1},t_{2}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in\overline{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})
satisfying (
5.46
), we use Lemma
5.2
to obtain that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}+c\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}E_{p,\tau}\left(u\,;\,{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}(x_{d},t_{2})\right)\right]

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. As in (

) along with Lemma
2.3
, (

) and (

), we deduce that

(6.16)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(x_{0})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0})}\big|^{2}}{(\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}})^{2s+2\tau}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})
is a compact set, there are finite mutually disjoint open sets
\mathcal{Q}_{r_{s}}(z_{1,i})
and
\mathcal{Q}_{r_{s}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{2,j})
for some points
z_{1,i},(x_{1,j},t_{2,j}),(x_{2,j},t_{2,j})\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})
such that

\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})\subset\left(\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{r_{s}}(z_{1,i})\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{r_{s}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{2,j})\right)\subset Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0}),

and
\mathcal{Q}_{r_{s}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{2,j})
satisfies (
5.46
). Combine (

) and (

) to see that

(6.17)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{R}\left(u,f,g,p_{0},\tilde{p},Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0}))\right).

Consequently, after a few simple calculations with Lemma

, we estimate the right-hand side of (

) to get that (
6.12
) holds. In addition, using the standard covering argument along with (
6.16
) and (
6.17
), we prove that
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{\tilde{p}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\,d\mu_{\tau,t};\Omega\times\Omega\times(0,T)\right)
. If

, then by recalling (

), (

), (

), (
6.2
), (

) and (

), we obtain
d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\,d\mu_{\tau,t};\Omega\times\Omega\times(0,T)\right)
and the desired estimate (
1.9
). Let us assume that
h>0
. We have shown that
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{p_{1}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\,d\mu_{\tau,t};\Omega\times\Omega\times(0,T)\right)
and (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) with

. Thus, by following the same line as in the proof for (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) with

replaced by
p_{1}
, we have that

(6.18)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{s}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(x_{0})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0})}\big|^{2}}{(\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}})^{2s+2\tau}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

for any
\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}=\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\times\Lambda_{\frac{r_{s}}{8\sqrt{n}}}(t_{2})
with
(x_{1},t_{2}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in\overline{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})
satisfying (
5.46
) and

(6.19)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau,t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{R}\left(u,f,g,p_{1},\tilde{p}_{1},Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0}))\right)

where
\tilde{p}_{1}
is the constant defined in (
6.4
) with

. As a result, plugging (
6.17
) to the first term in
\mathcal{R}\left(u,f,g,p_{1},\tilde{p}_{1},Q_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}}}(z_{0}))\right)
and then after a few simple calculations with Lemma
2.3
, we obtain (
6.12
) with

and
\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}_{1}
. In addition, using the standard covering argument along with (
6.18
) and (
6.19
), we prove that
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{\tilde{p}_{1}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\,d\mu_{\tau,t};\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\right)
. By iterating this procedure
l_{q}-1
times, we obtain
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\,d\mu_{\tau,t};\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\right)
with the estimate (
6.12
) with
\tilde{p}=q
. By recalling (
2.2
), (
3.5
), (
5.9
), (
6.2
), (
6.4
) and (
6.11
), we conclude that
d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\,d\mu_{\tau,t};\Omega\times\Omega_{T}\right)
with the desired estimate (
1.9
).
∎

We finish this section by proving Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

We note that in the proof of Lemma
6.1
and Theorem
1.1
, we only use the condition (
5.2
) to apply Fubini’s Theorem on the term
D^{\tau}d_{0}f
(see (
6.10
)). Taking into account Remark
5
, if
f=0
, then the constant
\tau
can be chosen in
(0,\min\{s,1-s\})
. Consequently, we allow for choosing
\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\min\{s,1-s\}\right)
considering (
6.11
) and we are able to prove the Theorem
1.2
by following the same lines as in the proof of Theorem
1.1
with
f=0
.
∎

Appendix A
self-improving property of nonlocal parabolic equations

In this appendix, we prove a self-improving property of a weak solution
u
to (
1.1
) with
f=g=0
. Throughout this section, we take

(A.1)

\tau_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{s}{2},\frac{1-s}{2}\right\}.

Before proving Lemma
4.1
, we are going to prove a reverse Hölder’s inequality on diagonal parts and obtain another covering lemma.

With the aid of the gluing lemma, we first obtain the following inequality.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
) and let
Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
.
Then for any
\varsigma\in(0,1]
, we have

(A.2)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\rho^{2s+2\tau}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\varsigma\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|^{2}}{\rho^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dx+\frac{c}{\varsigma^{\beta}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+c\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}+c\Tail_{1,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{\rho^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+c\Tail_{1,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{\rho^{\tau_{0}}};Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}+c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}\left((2\rho)^{s-\tau}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}

for some constants
c=c(n,s,L)
and
\beta=\beta(n,s)
, where the constant
\gamma
is defined in (
3.2
).

Proof.

We may assume that
z_{0}=0
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}|u-(u)_{Q_{\rho}}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}|u-(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)|^{2}\,dz+c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}|(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)-(u)_{Q_{\rho}}|^{2}\,dz\eqqcolon I_{1}+I_{2}.

In light of Lemma
2.1
with
h
,
p
and
s
, replaced by
u
,
\gamma
and
\tilde{s}\coloneqq s+\tau_{0}\left(1-\frac{2}{\gamma}\right)
, respectively, we first estimate
I_{1}
as

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}|u-(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{2}{\tilde{\gamma}}}\leq c\left(\frac{\rho^{\gamma(s+\tau_{0})}}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\tilde{\gamma}}}\times\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}}|u-(u)_{B_{\rho}(t)}|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{2\tilde{s}\gamma}{n\tilde{\gamma}}},

where
c=c(n,s)
and
\tilde{\gamma}\coloneqq\gamma\left(1+\frac{2\tilde{s}}{n}\right)
. We next apply Young’s inequality along with the fact that
\frac{\gamma}{\tilde{\gamma}}+\frac{2\tilde{s}\gamma}{n\tilde{\gamma}}=1
, in order to get

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq\vartheta\left(\frac{\rho^{\gamma(s+\tau_{0})}}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+\frac{c}{\vartheta^{\beta}}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}}|u-(u)_{B_{\rho}(t)}|^{2}\,dx

for any
\vartheta\in(0,1]
and for some constant
\beta=\beta(n,s)
. We now estimate
I_{2}
as

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{\Lambda_{\rho}\times\Lambda_{\rho}}|(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)-(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{\Lambda_{\rho}\times\Lambda_{\rho}}\left|(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)-(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t)\right|^{2}\,dt\,dt^{\prime}+c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{\Lambda_{\rho}\times\Lambda_{\rho}}\left|(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t)-(u)^{\psi}_{B_{\rho}}(t^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,dt\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle+c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-6.52774pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-5.09723pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.80557pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.05557pt}}\!\iint_{\Lambda_{\rho}\times\Lambda_{\rho}}\left|(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t^{\prime})-(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,dt\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}}\left|(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)-(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t)\right|^{2}\,dt+c\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in\Lambda_{\rho}}\left|(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t_{1})-(u)^{\psi}_{B_{\rho}}(t_{2})\right|^{2}\eqqcolon I_{2,1}+I_{2,2},

where
\psi
is the given function in Lemma
3.2
and

(u)_{B_{\rho}}^{\psi}(t)\equiv\frac{1}{\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}}\int_{B_{\rho}}u(x,t)\psi(x)\,dx.

Using the fact that
\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}\approx_{n}|B_{\rho}|
and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle I_{2,1}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\rho}}\left|\frac{1}{\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}}\int_{B_{\rho}}\left(u-(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)\right)\,dx\right|^{2}\,dt\leq cI_{1}

for some constant
c=c(n,s)
. Using Lemma
3.2
, we get that

\displaystyle I_{2,2}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2s-1}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{n+2s-1}}\,dy\,dz+c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{\rho}}\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|}{|y|^{n+2s}}\,dy\,dz

\displaystyle+c\rho^{2s-1}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{n+s-1}}\,dy\,dz+c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{\rho}}\frac{|f(x,t)-f(y,t)|}{|y|^{n+s}}\,dy\,dz

\displaystyle+c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\rho^{2s}|g|^{\gamma}\,dz\eqqcolon\sum_{i=1}^{5}I_{2,2,i}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
.
We now estimate
I_{2,2,1}
and
I_{2,2,2}
.

Estimate of
I_{2,2,1}
.
Using Hölder’s inequality and (
2.2
), we have

\displaystyle I_{2,2,1}

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2s-1}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}}{|x-y|^{n-2\tau}}\,dx\,dy\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n+\gamma^{\prime}\left(s-1+\tau_{0}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-1\right)\right)}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\rho^{s+\tau_{0}}}{1-(s+\tau_{0})}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}

Estimate of
I_{2,2,2}
.
A simple algebraic computation yields that

\displaystyle I_{2,2,2}

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{\rho}}\frac{|u(x,t)-(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)|}{|y|^{n+2s}}\,dy\,dz+c\rho^{2s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{\rho}}\frac{|(u)_{B_{\rho}}(t)-u(y,t)|}{|y|^{n+2s}}\,dy\,dz

\displaystyle\leq cI_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\Tail_{1,s}\left(u-(u)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t);Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right).

Similarly, we estimate
I_{2,2,3}
and
I_{2,2,4}
as

I_{2,2,3}+I_{2,2,4}\leq c\frac{\rho^{s+\tau_{0}}}{1-s}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\rho^{s}\Tail_{1,\frac{s}{2}}\left(f-(f)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t);Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right).

Using Hölder’s inequality, (
2.2
) and (
3.5
), we estimate
I_{2,2,5}
as

I_{2,2,5}\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}\left((2\rho)^{s-\tau_{0}}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}.

Take
\vartheta=\frac{\varsigma}{c}
for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
and combine all the above estimates to get (
A.2
).
∎

Remark 10
.

By estimating
I_{1}
in the proof of Lemma
A.1
as

I_{1}\leq\frac{c}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t},

we find that for any
\tau_{0}\in(0,\min\{s,1-s\})
, there holds

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{\rho}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\rho^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}+c\Tail_{1,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{\rho^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+c\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{0}f|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}+c\Tail_{1,s}\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{\rho^{\tau_{0}}};Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}\left((2\rho)^{s-\tau_{0}}|G|\right)^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
.

In light of Lemma
A.1
, we now prove the following reverse Hölder’s inequality.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
) with
f=g=0
and let
Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
. Then we have

\displaystyle E_{2,\tau_{0}}\left(u\,;\,Q_{\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}+c_{0}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{|u-(u)_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}(t)|}{(2\rho)^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2},

where
c_{0}=c_{0}(n,s,L)
and
E_{2,\tau_{0}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
5.32
).

Proof.

From (
2.2
) and (
3.1
) with
k=(u)_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}
,
f=0
and
g=0
, we deduce that

\displaystyle E_{2,\tau_{0}}(u\,;\,Q_{\rho})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{cr^{n+2}}{(r-\rho)^{n+2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-5.94443pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-4.68747pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.11249pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.4375pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{r^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dz+\frac{cr^{2n+4s}}{(r-\rho)^{2n+4s}}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(t)}{r^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{r}(z_{0})\right)^{2},

where
c=c(n,s,L)
. Using the estimate (
A.2
) with
\rho=r
,
f=0
and
g=0
into the first term in the right-hand side of the above inequality and Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle E_{2,\tau_{0}}(u\,;\,Q_{\rho})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}E_{2,\tau_{0}}(u\,;\,Q_{r})+\frac{cr^{n+2}}{(r-\rho)^{n+2}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{r}{r-\rho}\right)^{2(n+2s)}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}(t)}{(2\rho)^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{2\rho}(z_{0})\right)^{2}.

by taking
\varsigma\in(0,1)
sufficiently small depending only on
n,s
and
L
. Additionally, for the tail term, we have used Lemma
2.3
. Finally, employing Lemma
2.4
, we obtain the desired estimate (
3.6
).
∎

We next prove the following covering lemma.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2
and
u
be a weak solution to (
5.1
) with
f=g=0
. Then, there are two families of countable disjoint cylinders
\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{{i}}}(z_{i})\right\}_{i\geq 0}
and
\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{\widetilde{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\right\}_{j\geq 0}
, such that

(A.4)

U_{\lambda}=\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u(x,y,t)|\geq\lambda\right\}\subset\left(\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{{i}}}\left(z_{i}\right)\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup\limits_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\widetilde{r}_{{j}}}\left(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j}\right)\right)

whenever
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, where

(A.5)

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{5n}{s}}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{2}}|^{2}}{2^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{5n}{s}}}\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{2}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
. In particular, we have

(A.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geq 0}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{{i}}}(z_{i})\right)\leq\frac{c}{\lambda^{\gamma}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|>b_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t},

(A.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{r}_{{j}}}\left(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j}\right)\right)\leq\frac{c}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|>\frac{\lambda}{16}\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}++\sum_{i\geq 0}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{{i}}}(z_{i})\right)

for some constant
b_{u}=b_{u}(n,s,L)\in(0,1]
, where the constant
\gamma
is defined in (
3.2
), and we also have

(A.8)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\widetilde{r}_{{j}}}\left(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j}\right)}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2_{\#}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{{2_{\#}}}}\leq c\lambda\quad\text{for any }j,

where the constant
2_{\#}
is given in (
2.1
) with
p=2
.

Proof.

We first define a functional

\displaystyle\Theta_{D}\left(z_{0},r\right)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\tau_{0}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{r}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{r}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{r^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

for any
z_{0}\in Q_{r_{1}}
and
r>0
with
Q_{r}(z_{0})\subset Q_{2}
. Let us take

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{M\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa^{-1}}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{5n}{s}}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{M\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa^{-1}}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{5n}{s}}}\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{2}}|^{2}}{2^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
M\geq 1
and
\kappa\in(0,1]
are free parameters which will be determined later. We next take a positive integer
j_{0}=j_{0}(n,s,\Lambda)
such that

(A.9)

\frac{16(c_{0}+\tilde{c}+2c_{2})^{2}}{1-2^{-s+\tau_{0}}}\leq 2^{j_{0}\left(s-\tau_{0}\right)},

where
c_{0}
is the constant determined in Lemma
A.2
, and
\tilde{c}
and
c_{2}
are the constants determined in (
2.6
).
We then note that for any
z_{0}\in Q_{r_{1}}
,

Q_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\times 2^{j_{0}+2}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}}(z_{0})\subset Q_{r_{2}},

where
\mathcal{R}_{1,2}
is defined in (
5.14
).
Let us now define for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
,

D_{\kappa\lambda}=\left\{z_{0}\in Q_{r_{1}}\,:\,\sup_{0<\rho\leq\mathcal{R}_{1,2}}\Theta_{D}\left(z_{0},\rho\right)>\kappa\lambda\right\}.

We now take the constant
M
as in (
5.18
) with
q=2
. For any
z_{0}\in Q_{r_{1}}
and
r\in\left[\mathcal{R}_{1,2},5^{\frac{2}{s}}\times 2^{j_{0}+3}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}\right]
, we first note that

\Theta_{D}(z_{0},r)\leq\kappa\lambda,

by following the same line as in the proof for (
5.19
) with
p=2
.
Therefore, there is an exit radius
\rho_{z}
for each
z\in D_{\kappa\lambda}
such that

(A.10)

\Theta_{D}\left(z,\rho_{z}\right)\geq\kappa\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta_{D}\left(z,\rho\right)\leq\kappa\lambda\quad\text{ if }\rho_{z}\leq\rho\leq 5^{\frac{2}{s}}\times 2^{j_{0}+3}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}.

We now apply Vitali’s covering lemma to find a family of mutually disjoint countable cylinders

(A.11)

\left\{Q_{2^{j_{0}}\rho_{z_{i}}}(z_{i})\right\}_{i\geq 0}\text{ such that }D_{\kappa\lambda}\subset\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}Q_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\times 2^{j_{0}}\rho_{z_{i}}}(z_{i}).

We now denote

(A.12)

\rho_{i}=2^{j_{0}}\rho_{z_{i}}\quad\text{for each }i.

From (
A.10
), we have

(A.13)

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa\lambda}{\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{z_{i}}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{\rho_{z_{i}}}(t_{i})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho_{z_{i}}}(x_{i})}\frac{|u-(u)_{Q_{\rho_{z_{i}}}(z_{i})}|^{2}}{\rho_{z_{i}}^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

On account of Lemma
A.2
, Lemma
2.6
and (
A.12
), we estimate the right-hand side of (
A.13
) as

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa\lambda}{\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}+c_{0}\tilde{c}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{j=j_{0}+1}^{l}2^{i\left(-s+\tau_{0}\right)}\Theta_{D}\left(z_{j},2^{j}\rho_{z_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+c_{0}\tilde{c}\left(\frac{2}{2^{j_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}}\right)^{s+\tau_{0}}\left(\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)|^{2}}{2^{2s+2\tau_{0}}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{c_{0}\tilde{c}}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{5n}}\Tail_{\gamma,2s}\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{s+\tau_{0}}};Q_{2}\right)

where
c=c(n,s,L)
and
l
is the positive integer such that
2^{j_{0}+1}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}\leq 2^{l}\rho_{z_{i}}<2^{j_{0}+2}\mathcal{R}_{1,2}.

For the detailed calculations of the above inequality, we refer to (
5.25
) and (
5.27
) with
f=0
and
g=0
.
As a result, using (
A.9
) and (
A.10
), we find that

\frac{\kappa\lambda}{{\tau_{0}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
. A suitable choice of the constant
\tilde{b}_{u}=\tilde{b}_{u}(n,s,L)\in\left(0,\frac{1}{8}\right]
yields

(A.14)

\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\right)\leq\frac{c}{(\kappa\lambda)^{\gamma}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|>\tilde{b}_{u}\kappa\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\gamma}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t},

as the constant
\tau_{0}
depends only on
s
(see (
A.1
)).
With (
A.10
) and (
A.11
), we follow the same lines as in the proof of step 2 through step 8 given in Lemma
5.1
with
p=2
,
f=0
and
g=0
. As a result, by taking
\kappa
as in (
5.36
) with
\tau^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}
replaced by
\tau_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we find that there is a collection of countable disjoint cylinders

\{\mathcal{Q}\}_{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}}
such that

\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u(x,y,t)|\geq\lambda\right\}\subset\left(\bigcup\limits_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{{i}}}(z_{i})\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup\limits_{j\geq 0}\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\widetilde{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\right)\leq\frac{c}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|>\frac{\lambda}{16}\right\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}+c\sum_{i}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{i}}(z_{i})\right)

and

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\widetilde{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2_{\#}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{{2_{\#}}}}\leq c\lambda\quad\text{for any }j,

where
c=c(n,s,L)
(see (
5.67
)and (
5.49
) for the second and the third inequalities, respectively).
Let
b_{u}=\tilde{b}_{u}\kappa
. Then the above three observations and (
A.14
) yield the desired results as the constant
\tau_{0}
depends only on
s
. This completes the proof.
∎

We are now in the position to prove the following self-improving property for a weak solution to the corresponding homogeneous problem of (
1.1
).

Theorem A.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
) with
f=0
and
g=0
. Then there are constants
\epsilon=\epsilon(n,s,L)\in(0,1)
and
c=c(n,s,L)
such that

(A.15)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2+\epsilon}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2r}(z_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}{u}|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2r}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.4965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.23955pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.45624pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-1.6875pt}}\!\int_{B_{2r}}\frac{|{u}-({u})_{Q_{2r}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{(2r)^{2s+2\tau}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+c\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{{u}-({u})_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}(t)}{(2r)^{s+\tau}};Q_{2r}(z_{0})\right)

whenever
Q_{2r}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
.

Proof.

Let us fix
Q_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\Omega_{T}
. We now define for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
t\in\Lambda_{2}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,

\tilde{u}(x,t)=\frac{u\left({r}x+x_{0},{r}^{2s}t+t_{0}\right)}{{r}^{s+\tau}},\,\tilde{A}(x,y,t)=A\left({r}x+x_{0},{r}y+x_{0},{r}^{2s}t+t_{0}\right)\text{ and }\tilde{\Phi}(\xi)=\frac{\tilde{\Phi}\left({r}^{\tau}\xi\right)}{{r}^{\tau}}

to see that
\tilde{u}
is a weak solution to (
5.1
) with
f=g=0
,
A=\tilde{A}
and
\Phi=\tilde{\Phi}
.
Let us take
\epsilon\in\left(0,\frac{2_{\#}-2}{2}\right)
which will be determined later. For each
N>0
, we now define
\phi_{N}:[1,2]\to\mathbb{R}
by

\phi_{N}(\rho)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\rho}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2+\epsilon}}.

For
\lambda_{0}
as defined in (
A.5
) with
u=\tilde{u}
, we claim that if
N>\lambda_{0}
, then there is a constant
c=c(n,s,L)
such that for any
1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2
,

(A.16)

\phi_{N}(r_{1})\leq\frac{1}{2}\phi_{N}(r_{2})+c\lambda_{0}.

By Fubini’s theorem, we observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\nu\left(\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}>\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}}^{N}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\nu\left(\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}>\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda\eqqcolon I+J,

where
d\,\nu=|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}
and
N>\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}
with
\mathcal{M}>1
to be determined later. We first estimate
I
as

\displaystyle I\leq c\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda_{0}^{\epsilon}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\leq c\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda_{0}^{2+\epsilon}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{2}\right),

where
c=c(n,s,L)
. We next estimate
J
as

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\nu\left(\left\{(x,y,t)\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\geq 0}\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\nu\left(\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{{i}}}\left(z_{i}\right)\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 0}\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\nu\left(\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2},

where we have used the change of variables and (
A.4
). In light of the definition of the measure
\nu
, (
A.10
), (
2.3
) and (
A.6
), we estimate
J_{1}
as

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{2}{s}}\rho_{{i}}}\left(z_{i}\right)}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{i}\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\lambda^{2}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho_{{i}}}\left(z_{i}\right)\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\epsilon\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}}{\lambda^{\gamma-(1+\epsilon)}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|\geq b_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{\gamma}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda,

where
c=c(n,s,L)
and the constant
b_{u}
is determined in Lemma
A.3
. Using Fubini’s theorem, we get

J_{1}\leq c\epsilon\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t},

where
c=c(n,s,L)
, as
\frac{1}{2-\gamma}>0
depends only on
n
and
s
.
To estimate
J_{2}
, we first note from the weak 1-1 estimate and (
A.8
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\nu\left(\left\{\mathcal{Q}\,:\,|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}>\mathcal{M}\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\int_{\mathcal{Q}}\frac{|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}^{2_{\#}-2}}{(\mathcal{M}\lambda)^{2_{\#}-2}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon+2-2_{\#}}\lambda^{\epsilon+1}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)\,d\lambda,

where we denote
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}_{5^{\frac{1}{s}}\overline{r}_{j}}(x_{1,j},x_{2,j},t_{0,j})
. We first note

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{\gamma}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|\geq b_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{\gamma}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2-\gamma}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|\geq b_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{\gamma}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|\geq b_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
as
\gamma<2
and the constant
b_{u}
depends only on
n,s
and
L
.
Considering the above two inequalities, (
A.7
) and the estimate
J_{1}
, we estimate
J_{2}
as

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon+2-2_{\#}}\lambda^{\epsilon-1}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}\cap\{|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|\geq b_{u}\lambda\}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon+2-2_{\#}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{10n}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}

by taking
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,s,L)>1
sufficiently large so that
c\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2-2_{\#}}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{10n}}
(thanks to
\mathcal{M}^{\epsilon+2-2_{\#}}\leq\mathcal{M}^{\frac{2-2_{\#}}{2}}
as
\mathcal{M}\geq 1
and
\epsilon<\frac{2_{\#}-2}{2}
). We next select
\epsilon=\epsilon(n,s,L)<1
such that

J_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2^{10n}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}.

Combining all the estimates of
I
and
J
, we observe that

\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|_{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\leq\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|_{N}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\leq\frac{1}{2^{9n}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|_{N\mathcal{M}^{-1}}^{2+\epsilon}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}+c\lambda_{0}^{2+\epsilon}\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\left(\mathcal{Q}_{2}\right)

holds if
N>\mathcal{M}\lambda_{0}
. After a few algebraic computations along with (
2.2
), we have (
A.16
). Applying Lemma
2.4
to (
A.16
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|_{N}^{2+\epsilon}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{2}d\mu_{\tau_{0},t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c\Tail_{\infty,2s}\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{2}}(t)}{2^{s+\tau}};Q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+c\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{2}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\frac{|\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{Q_{2}}|^{2}}{2^{2s+2\tau}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L)
. By passing to the limit
N\to\infty

and using the change of variables, we get the desired estimate (
A.15
).
∎

Appendix B
Existence and uniqueness

In this section, we present the existence result for the corresponding boundary value problem of (
1.1
) and the standard energy estimate. Before stating the result, we first note from
[
47
, Proposition 1.2 in Chapter 3]
with
V=W^{s,2}(\Omega)
and
H=L^{2}(\Omega)
that if
h\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega))
and
h_{t}\in\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega)\right)^{*}
, then
h\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
with the estimate

(B.1)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq c\|h\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega))}+c\|h_{t}\|_{\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega)\right)^{*}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s)
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}
be an open and bounded set such that
\Omega\Subset\Omega^{\prime}
. Suppose that

(B.2)

\displaystyle f\in L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)\quad\text{with}\quad d_{0}f\in L^{2}\left(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime}\times(0,T)\,;\,\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\right),

\displaystyle g\in L^{\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}}(\Omega_{T}),

\displaystyle h\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega^{\prime}))\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}_{2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\quad\text{and}\quad h_{t}\in\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega^{\prime}))\right)^{*}.

Then there is a unique weak solution
u\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega))\cap L^{\infty}\left(0,T;L^{1}_{2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)\cap C([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
to

(B.3)

\left\{\begin{aligned} u_{t}+\mathcal{L}^{\Phi}_{A}u&=(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f+g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\Omega\times(0,T)$},\\
u&=h&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Omega\times[0,T]$},\\
u(\cdot,0)&=h(\cdot,0)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\Omega$}\end{aligned}\right.

with the estimate

(B.4)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}|u(x,t)|^{2}\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|d_{s}u|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{0}f|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\Tail_{2,s}(f-(f)_{\Omega^{\prime}}(t);\Omega^{\prime}_{T})^{2}+c\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|g|^{\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{n+4s}{n+2s}}

\displaystyle+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{s}h|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\Tail_{2,2s}(h-(h)_{\Omega^{\prime}}(t);\Omega^{\prime}_{T})^{2}+c\|h_{t}\|^{2}_{\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega^{\prime})\right)^{*}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,T,\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})
.

Proof.

From
[
13
, Lemma 2.7]
, we observe that

T_{f(\cdot,t)}:\phi\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(f(x,t)-f(y,t))(\phi(x)-\phi(y))\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n+s}},\quad\phi\in X_{0}^{s,2}(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})

is an element of the dual space of
X_{0}^{s,2}(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})
. This implies that

(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\in\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega^{\prime})\right)^{*}.

Therefore, combining
[
10
, Thoerem A.3]
and
[
11
, Lemma A.1]
, we find a unique weak solution
u
to (
B.3
).

We are now in the position to prove (
B.4
). Since
u-h\in L^{2}\left(0,T;X_{0}^{s,2}(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})\right)
, using the standard approximation argument, we have

(B.5)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}|(u-h)(x,t)|^{2}\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|d_{s}(u-h)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left|\int_{0}^{T}\langle h_{t},u-h\rangle_{X_{0}^{s,2}(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}),\left(X_{0}^{s,2}(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})\right)^{*}}\,dt\right|+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|g(u-h)|\,dz

\displaystyle+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|d_{0}f||d_{s}(u-h)|\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|d_{s}h||d_{s}(u-h)|\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}\eqqcolon I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

From (
B.2
) and Young’s inequality, we first estimate
I_{1}
as

I_{1}\leq c\|h_{t}\|^{2}_{\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega^{\prime})\right)^{*}}+\frac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{s}(u-h)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}.

We next estimate
I_{2}
with the help of (
B.2
) as below

I_{2}\leq c\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|g|^{\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{n+4s}{n+2s}}+\frac{1}{8}\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}|(u-h)(x,t)|^{2}\,dx+\frac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{s}(u-h)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}},

where we have used Lemma
2.1
and Young’s inequality.
For the estimate of
I_{3}+I_{4}
, we follow the same line as in the estimate of
J_{1}+J_{2}
in
[
13
, Lemma 2.7]
to see that

\displaystyle I_{3}+I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{s}(u-h)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{0}f|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\Tail_{2,s}(f-(f)_{\Omega^{\prime}}(t);\Omega^{\prime}_{T})^{2}

\displaystyle+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{s}h|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\Tail_{2,2s}(h-(h)_{\Omega^{\prime}}(t);\Omega^{\prime}_{T})^{2}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,L,\Omega,\Omega^{\prime},T)
. We now plug the estimates of
I_{1},I_{2}
and
I_{3}
into (
B.5
) to see that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}|(u-h)(x,t)|^{2}\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|d_{s}(u-h)|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{0}f|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy\,dt}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\Tail_{2,s}(f-(f)_{\Omega^{\prime}}(t);\Omega^{\prime}_{T})^{2}+c\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|g|^{\frac{2(n+2s)}{n+4s}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{n+4s}{n+2s}}

\displaystyle+c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|d_{s}h|^{2}\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n}}+c\Tail_{2,2s}(h-(h)_{\Omega^{\prime}}(t);\Omega^{\prime}_{T})^{2}+c\|h_{t}\|^{2}_{\left(L^{2}(0,T;W^{s,2}(\Omega^{\prime})\right)^{*}}.

After a few simple calculations along with (
B.1
), we obtain the desired estimate (
B.4
).
∎
